**Rory Daniel McIlroy** (born 4 May 1989) is a Northern Irish [professional golfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_golfer) who plays on the [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour) and the [European Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Tour). He is a former [world number one](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Number_One_male_golfers) in the [Official World Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_World_Golf_Ranking) and has spent over 100 weeks in that position during his career. A six-time [major champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men's_major_golf_championships), he is the sixth man to complete the modern [career grand slam](224580.md)—after [Gene Sarazen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Sarazen), [Ben Hogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Hogan), [Gary Player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Player), [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus), and [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods)—and the first European to do so.

McIlroy reached number one in the [World Amateur Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Amateur_Golf_Ranking) in 2007 and turned professional later that year. He won his first major championship at the [2011 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_U.S._Open_\(golf\)) and rose to number one in the Official World Golf Ranking in 2012. By age 25, McIlroy had won three more majors: the [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_PGA_Championship) and [2014 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_PGA_Championship) and the [2014 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Open_Championship). In 2022, he became the first person to win the [FedEx Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Cup) three times. McIlroy won [The Players Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Players_Championship) in 2019 and 2025. After a ten-year drought in the majors, he won his fifth at the [2025 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Masters_Tournament) to complete the career grand slam; he added a second consecutive Masters in [2026](82215987.md).&#x20;

McIlroy has represented Europe, Ireland and Great Britain & Ireland at amateur and professional level. He has played for Europe at the [Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryder_Cup) in every edition from 2010 to 2025, with Europe winning in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Ryder_Cup), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Ryder_Cup), [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Ryder_Cup), [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Ryder_Cup), [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ryder_Cup) and [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Ryder_Cup). McIlroy has been named [PGA Tour Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour_Player_of_the_Year) three times and has won the European Tour's [Harry Vardon Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Vardon_Trophy#Winners) seven times.

## Early life

Rory Daniel McIlroy was born on 4 May 1989 in [Holywood, County Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holywood,_County_Down), Northern Ireland.[^cite_note-5] His mother, Rosaleen "Rosie" McDonald, was originally from [Lurgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lurgan), County Armagh, while his father, Gerry McIlroy, was from Holywood.[^cite_note-\:0-6] His parents met while Rosie worked as a waitress at a bar Gerry managed in [Belfast](5046.md) and they married at St Colmcille's Church in Holywood in January 1988, both aged 27.[^cite_note-\:9-7] McIlroy was an only child and the family lived in a modest, [semi-detached](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-detached) house in Holywood.[^cite_note-\:0-6] He attended St Patrick's, a Catholic primary school, until he passed his [eleven-plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleven-plus) and gained entry to [Sullivan Upper School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sullivan_Upper_School), a [mixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_education_in_Northern_Ireland) grammar school.[^cite_note-\:0-6][^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9]



Rory and father Gerry McIlroy in May 2013

McIlroy was introduced to golf at a young age by his father and received a set of plastic clubs when he was two years old. His father was a golfer himself, who once played at a [scratch handicap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handicap_\(golf\)) level.[^cite_note-\:0-6] On his mother's side, McIlroy's uncle Mickey McDonald was a talented athlete: he played [Gaelic football](37688.md) for [Armagh GAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armagh_GAA), winning the [Ulster Senior Football Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulster_Senior_Football_Championship) in 1982, and [association football](10568.md) for [Glenavon F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenavon_F.C.) and [Cliftonville F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliftonville_F.C.).[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11]

McIlroy regularly asked his father to take him to the nearby Holywood Golf Club, where he gained attention by hitting 40-yard (37 m) drives at age three. He practiced [chipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chip_\(golf\)) at home by hitting balls into the family's washing machine, studied a golf technique video by [Nick Faldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Faldo) and often went to sleep holding a golf club to develop [muscle memory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muscle_memory) of the interlocking grip. At age seven, McIlroy became Holywood Golf Club's youngest-ever member and dreamed of becoming a professional golfer.[^cite_note-SiebertNYT-12] He was a self-described "[anorak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anorak_\(slang\))" of [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) as a child, stating "I was only eight but I watched every hole" of Woods's victory at the [1997 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Masters_Tournament).[^cite_note-13] McIlroy received early tutelage from coach [Michael Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Bannon) at Holywood Golf Club.[^cite_note-\:0-6]

To fund his golfing ambitions, McIlroy's parents took on extra jobs.[^cite_note-\:0-6] Gerry worked 100 hours a week; he cleaned toilets and showers at a local sports club in the mornings, served as a bartender at Holywood Golf Club from 12 to 6 pm, then returned to the sports club to work behind the bar in the evenings. Rosie looked after Rory during the day and worked night shifts packaging rolls of tape at a [3M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3M) factory in [Bangor, County Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangor,_County_Down).[^cite_note-\:9-7][^cite_note-14][^cite_note-15] Due to their conflicting schedules, McIlroy's parents rarely saw each other during this period.[^cite_note-\:0-6] Gerry later said: "I had no idea what else to do. I'm a working-class man. We wanted to give our only child a chance."[^cite_note-16] After finding success as a professional golfer, McIlroy bought his parents a house in 2009, stating: "I'll never be able to repay Mum and Dad for what they did, but at least they know they'll never have to work another day. I'll do whatever it takes to look after them."[^cite_note-\:0-6]

Aged nine, McIlroy had his first significant international amateur tournament victory, at the U10 World Championship held at [Doral Golf Resort & Spa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doral_Golf_Resort_&_Spa) in [Miami](53846.md), Florida.[^cite_note-17] After this win, he was invited by broadcaster [Gerry Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerry_Kelly_\(broadcaster\)) to appear on the television show *[Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelly_\(talk_show\))*, where he demonstrated his technique of chipping a golf ball into a washing machine for the audience.[^cite_note-\:0-6][^cite_note-18] In a 1998 interview with [BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport), McIlroy said his ambition was to win all four [major championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men's_major_golf_championships) titles.[^cite_note-19] He became a scratch handicap at age 12,[^cite_note-20] and began to receive mentorship from professional golfer [Darren Clarke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darren_Clarke).[^cite_note-21] McIlroy decided to leave school in 2005 in order to concentrate on golf.[^cite_note-22]

## Amateur career

McIlroy won the Ulster Boys' U15 Championship in 2002 and the Ulster Boys' U18 Championship in 2003.[^cite_note-rise-23] At the age of 15, he was a member of the Irish team at the 2004 [European Boys' Team Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Boys'_Team_Championship) in Finland and the European team which won the 2004 [Junior Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_Ryder_Cup) against the United States on foreign soil in [Ohio](22199.md).[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-EGABoys-25][^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] In 2005, McIlroy became the youngest-ever winner of both the West of Ireland Championship and the Irish Close Championship.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29] He had signed a [letter of intent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Letter_of_Intent) in late 2004 to play collegiate golf at [East Tennessee State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tennessee_State_University), but after his wins in 2005, he decided to forgo the golf scholarship and continue to play amateur golf in Europe.[^cite_note-30]

At age 16, McIlroy made his [European Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Tour) debut in May 2005 at the [Daily Telegraph Dunlop Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Telegraph_Dunlop_Masters).[^cite_note-31] Two months later, he shot a 61 at the Dunluce Links of [Royal Portrush Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Portrush_Golf_Club) during stroke-play qualifying at the North of Ireland Amateur Open,[^cite_note-32] breaking the competitive course record.[^cite_note-33] McIlroy made his first cut in a professional tournament at the [Morson International Pro-Am Challenge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morson_International_Pro-Am_Challenge) on the [Challenge Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Challenge_Tour) in August 2005.[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] He shot a final-round 67 to finish at 278, seven strokes behind the winner [Andrés Romero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Romero).[^cite_note-36]

McIlroy represented [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golfing_Union_of_Ireland) at the [2005 European Amateur Team Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_European_Amateur_Team_Championship). However, Ireland did not advance to the match-play portion.[^cite_note-37] Two years later, he competed for Ireland again at the [2007 European Amateur Team Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_European_Amateur_Team_Championship), this time with future Open champion [Shane Lowry](22834302.md) as one of his teammates. McIlroy was the individual leader in the stroke-play portion and Ireland ultimately defeated France in the final to claim victory. This was Ireland's first title in the championship since [1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_European_Amateur_Team_Championship).[^cite_note-38]

In August 2006, McIlroy won the [European Amateur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Amateur) at Biella Golf Club, near [Milan](36511.md), Italy, which earned him an exemption to [The Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Open_Championship) the following year.[^cite_note-39] Also in 2006, he represented Europe against the Asia/Pacific team at the [Bonallack Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonallack_Trophy) in [New Zealand](4913064.md),[^cite_note-40] represented Ireland in the [Eisenhower Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eisenhower_Trophy),[^cite_note-41] and retained both his West of Ireland and Irish Close titles.[^cite_note-top-42] McIlroy made his first cut on the European Tour in February 2007, as a 17-year-old at the [Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Desert_Classic). He had to forego prize money of over €7,000 due to his amateur status.[^cite_note-43] Following his performance in Dubai, McIlroy rose to number one in the [World Amateur Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Amateur_Golf_Ranking).[^cite_note-top-42][^cite_note-44] In March, he won the [Grey Goose Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Nations_Cup_%E2%80%93_Copa_Sotogrande) in [Sotogrande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sotogrande), Spain, defeating [Marius Thorp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marius_Thorp) in a playoff.[^cite_note-45]

In his [major championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men's_major_golf_championships) debut, at the [2007 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Open_Championship) held at [Carnoustie Golf Links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnoustie_Golf_Links) in July, McIlroy shot a bogey-free opening round of 3-under-par 68. He ended the tournament in tied-42nd place, ranking as the low amateur and winning the [silver medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Open_Championship#Silver_Medal_winners).[^cite_note-46] McIlroy chose to end his amateur career at the [2007 Walker Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Walker_Cup) in September, where he represented the Great Britain & Ireland team at the [Royal County Down Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_County_Down_Golf_Club).[^cite_note-47] McIlroy's overall record was 1–2–1, including a win in his Sunday singles match against [Billy Horschel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Horschel), as the United States emerged victorious over Great Britain & Ireland by a score of 12.5–11.5.[^cite_note-48]

## Professional career

### 2007–2009: Early years, first European Tour win

McIlroy turned professional on 18 September 2007 and signed with [International Sports Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Sports_Management). He made his first professional start the following day at the [Quinn Direct British Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinn_Direct_British_Masters),[^cite_note-49] where he finished in a tie for 42nd place.[^cite_note-50] In his next start, he finished third at the [Alfred Dunhill Links Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Dunhill_Links_Championship). He secured his European Tour card for 2008 the following week by finishing in a tie for 4th place at the [Open de Madrid Valle Romano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_de_Madrid_Valle_Romano).[^cite_note-monstersandcritics1-51] Aged 18, he became the youngest affiliate member in the history of the European Tour to earn a tour card.[^cite_note-52] On the 2007 European Tour season, he earned €277,255 and finished in 95th place on the Order of Merit list.[^cite_note-sportinglife1-53]

McIlroy entered the top 200 of the [Official World Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_World_Golf_Ranking) (OWGR) for the first time on 27 January 2008.[^cite_note-54] On 7 September 2008, McIlroy took a four-stroke lead into the final round of the [Omega European Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_European_Masters) in [Crans-sur-Sierre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crans-sur-Sierre), Switzerland, but finished in a tie for first place with Frenchman [Jean-François Lucquin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_Lucquin) and lost in a play-off.[^cite_note-55] McIlroy finished the [2008 European Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_European_Tour) season with six top-10 placements and ranked 79th in the OWGR.[^cite_note-56] After finishing second in the [UBS Hong Kong Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBS_Hong_Kong_Open) in November 2008,[^cite_note-57] McIlroy moved into the top 50 of the OWGR.[^cite_note-58] He ended the calendar year at 39th;[^cite_note-59] this earned him an invitation to the [2009 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Masters_Tournament).&#x20;

McIlroy's first professional win came at age 19 when he won the [Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Desert_Classic) on 1 February 2009, which took him to 16th in the OWGR.[^cite_note-60] At the [2009 WGC-Accenture Match Play Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_WGC-Accenture_Match_Play_Championship) in March, McIlroy reached the quarterfinals, where he lost to eventual champion [Geoff Ogilvy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoff_Ogilvy).[^cite_note-61] In April, McIlroy made his Masters debut, which was his first major championship as a professional. He finished the tournament tied for 20th place. Of the players to make the cut, he ranked third in average driving distance, beaten only by [Dustin Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dustin_Johnson) and [Andrés Romero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Romero).[^cite_note-62]

McIlroy then returned to Europe, finishing fifth at the [BMW PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_PGA_Championship) and 12th at the [European Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Open_\(golf\)) in May.[^cite_note-et_results09-63] He played in his second major as a professional at the [2009 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_U.S._Open_\(golf\)), where he shot a final-round 68 to finish in a tie for 10th.[^cite_note-et_results09-63] In July, McIlroy played in the [2009 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Open_Championship), his first Open as a professional, and finished T-47.[^cite_note-pgatour_results-64] The following month, he tied for 3rd on debut at the [2009 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_PGA_Championship).[^cite_note-pgatour_results-64] McIlroy finished the 2009 season ranked second on the [Race to Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_to_Dubai), behind [Lee Westwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Westwood), and in November he entered the top 10 of the world rankings for the first time.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66] McIlroy subsequently announced that he would join the American-based [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour) for the 2010 season.[^cite_note-67] He ended 2009 by representing Ireland at the [2009 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_World_Cup_\(men's_golf\)), alongside [Graeme McDowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graeme_McDowell). They opened with a 14-under 58 in the first-round [fourballs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-ball_golf),[^cite_note-68] and held a three-shot lead after the third round, but a final-round 70 resulted in a tie for 2nd.[^cite_note-69]

### 2010: First PGA Tour victory, Ryder Cup debut



McIlroy during a practice round of the [2010 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_PGA_Championship)

McIlroy started the year by finishing third at the 2010 [Abu Dhabi Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_Golf_Championship) in January. As the defending champion at the [Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Desert_Classic), he shot a final-round 73 to tie for fifth place.[^cite_note-70] On 2 May, McIlroy shot a course-record 62 in the final round of the [Quail Hollow Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quail_Hollow_Championship) to beat [Phil Mickelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Mickelson) by four strokes and record his first PGA Tour win.[^cite_note-71] The win came two days before McIlroy's 21st birthday, making him the first player since [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) to win a PGA Tour event prior to that age.[^cite_note-72] The win earned him a two-year exemption on the PGA Tour.[^cite_note-73]

At the [2010 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Open_Championship), held at the [Old Course at St Andrews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Course_at_St_Andrews) in July, McIlroy tied the course record with a 9-under-par 63 on the opening day.[^cite_note-74] He ended the tournament tied for third.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] In August, McIlroy finished in third-place at the [2010 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_PGA_Championship), one shot outside of a playoff between [Bubba Watson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bubba_Watson) and eventual winner [Martin Kaymer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Kaymer).[^cite_note-monstersandcritics1-51]

As an automatic qualifier, McIlroy was selected to represented Europe at the [2010 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Ryder_Cup). He tied his Sunday singles match against [Stewart Cink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stewart_Cink), helping Europe to regain the cup by a score of 14.5–13.5.[^cite_note-sportinglife1-53] Following the Ryder Cup, he announced in November that he would return to play full-time on the European Tour, although adding that he would continue to play a limited schedule on the PGA Tour.[^cite_note-77] McIlroy stated in 2012 that he regretted this 2010 decision to give up his PGA Tour card and skip the [2011 Players Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Players_Championship). McIlroy's manager [Chubby Chandler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Chandler_\(golfer\))'s aversion to the PGA Tour was cited by McIlroy as one of the main reasons for their later professional split.[^cite_note-GuardApr12-78]

### 2011: Masters collapse, U.S. Open breakthrough



McIlroy in June 2011 at the [Memorial Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Tournament)

In his first start of the year, McIlroy finished runner-up at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Golf_Championship) in January, eight strokes behind [Martin Kaymer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Kaymer).[^cite_note-79] In April, McIlroy shot a bogey-free 65 in the first round of the [2011 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Masters_Tournament) to lead the field.[^cite_note-80] This made him the youngest player at the time ever to hold the first-round lead at the Masters.[^cite_note-81] He then shot rounds of 69 and 70, giving him a four-stroke lead after 54 holes. In the final round, McIlroy shot an 8-over-par 80, including a triple bogey on the 10th and double bogey on the 12th hole. He ultimately finished in a tie for 15th, 10 strokes behind winner [Charl Schwartzel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charl_Schwartzel).[^cite_note-pgatour_results-64]

In his next major start, at the [2011 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_U.S._Open_\(golf\)) held at [Congressional Country Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Country_Club) in June, McIlroy again shot a bogey-free 65 to take the first-round lead. He shot 66 in the second round to set a record for the lowest 36-hole total in U.S. Open history (131, −11).[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] He posted 68 in third round, to also set the 54-hole scoring record at 199 (−14).[^cite_note-84] In doing so, he built an eight-stroke lead going into the final round. McIlroy shot a final round of 69 to earn his first major championship title, eight strokes ahead of runner-up [Jason Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Day).[^cite_note-85] McIlroy's 72-hole aggregate score of 268 (−16) was a new U.S. Open record, breaking the previous record of 272 jointly held by [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) ([1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_U.S._Open_\(golf\))), [Lee Janzen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Janzen) ([1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_U.S._Open_\(golf\))), [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) ([2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_U.S._Open_\(golf\))) and [Jim Furyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Furyk) ([2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_U.S._Open_\(golf\))).[^cite_note-86] The 16-under in relation to par beat Tiger Woods's 12-under at [Pebble Beach Golf Links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pebble_Beach_Golf_Links) in 2000.[^cite_note-seattle-pi-87][^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] At age 22, McIlroy also became the youngest winner since [Bobby Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Jones_\(golfer\)) in [1923 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1923_U.S._Open_\(golf\)), and the victory lifted him to a new career-high fourth in the OWGR.[^cite_note-90]

At the [2011 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Open_Championship) in July, he struggled in the tough weather conditions and finished in tied-25th place.[^cite_note-91] He was also a non-factor at the [2011 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_PGA_Championship), where he injured his wrist in the first round after attempting to play a stroke from behind a tree root.[^cite_note-92] In October, McIlroy won the [Lake Malaren Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Malaren_Shanghai_Masters) in a playoff against [Anthony Kim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Kim), earning the \$2 million first-place prize, the highest in golf at that time.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] In December, he won the [UBS Hong Kong Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Open_\(golf\)) by two strokes.[^cite_note-95]

### 2012: World number one, five-win season, second major championship title

In January, McIlroy finished solo-second at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Golf_Championship), one stroke behind winner [Robert Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Rock). At the [2012 WGC-Accenture Match Play Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_WGC-Accenture_Match_Play_Championship) in February, he recorded another runner-up finish, losing to [Hunter Mahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Mahan), 2 and 1, in the final. The following week, McIlroy won the [Honda Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_Classic), where he shot a final round of 69 to finish two strokes ahead of [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) and [Tom Gillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Gillis).[^cite_note-96] With this victory, McIlroy reached the [number one position](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_world_number_one_male_golfers) in the [Official World Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_World_Golf_Ranking) for the first time in his career. Aged 22, he became the second-youngest man to reach number one, behind Woods who achieved the feat at 21.[^cite_note-97]



McIlroy warming up at the [2012 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_U.S._Open_\(golf\)) in [San Francisco](49728.md)

At the [2012 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Masters_Tournament) in April, McIlroy was in third place after two rounds, one stroke behind the leaders, but struggled on the weekend with rounds of 77 and 76 and finished in a tie for 40th. In May, he was in contention at the [Wells Fargo Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo_Championship) and went to a playoff with [Rickie Fowler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rickie_Fowler) and [D. A. Points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D._A._Points). Fowler won the tournament on the first playoff hole with a birdie.[^cite_note-98]

In August, McIlroy won the [2012 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_PGA_Championship) at the [Kiawah Island Ocean Course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiawah_Island_Ocean_Course). He [birdied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birdie_\(golf\)) the final hole to shoot 66 and win by eight strokes, a new record for the largest margin-of-victory at the PGA Championship.[^cite_note-LATIMES-99][^cite_note-NYTIMES-100][^cite_note-GUARDIAN-101] The prior record had stood since [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) won the [1980 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_PGA_Championship) by seven strokes.[^cite_note-LATIMES-99][^cite_note-NYTIMES-100][^cite_note-GUARDIAN-101] At age 23, McIlroy became the youngest multiple major champion since [Seve Ballesteros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seve_Ballesteros) won the [1980 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Masters_Tournament).

At the [Deutsche Bank Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank_Championship) in September, McIlroy overcame a three-shot deficit headed into the final round to defeat [Louis Oosthuizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Oosthuizen) by one stroke.[^cite_note-102] The following week. McIlroy won the [BMW Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Championship_\(PGA_Tour\)), two strokes ahead of [Phil Mickelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Mickelson) and [Lee Westwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Westwood).[^cite_note-103] He totalled 40-under par across the two tournament victories.[^cite_note-104] With these wins, he became the first European to win four PGA Tour events in a single season and the only person other than [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) to win four events in a season since 2005.

McIlroy topped the qualification standings for the [2012 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Ryder_Cup), which was held at [Medinah Country Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medinah_Country_Club) at the end of September.[^cite_note-rc2012-105] He picked up three points for the European team.[^cite_note-rc2012-105] On the final day, a mix up with his tee time meant that he arrived only 12 minutes before he was due to tee off in his Sunday singles match against [Keegan Bradley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keegan_Bradley), after being escorted in by a police officer. He defeated Bradley, 2 and 1, to help Europe record a comeback win by a score of 14.5–13.5, in what became known as the "Miracle at Medinah".[^cite_note-106] To finish off his season, he won the [DP World Tour Championship, Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship,_Dubai) in November, making birdie on the last five holes to beat [Justin Rose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Rose) by two strokes.[^cite_note-107]

### 2013: Equipment change, downturn in form



McIlroy at the [2013 BMW PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_BMW_PGA_Championship)&#x20;

McIlroy struggled with an equipment change at the beginning of 2013, having signed a lucrative endorsement deal with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) in January. He missed the cut at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Golf_Championship) in January and withdrew from the Honda Classic as the defending champion in February.[^cite_note-108]

In April, he finished runner-up at the [Valero Texas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valero_Texas_Open), two strokes behind [Martin Laird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Laird), and at the [2013 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Masters_Tournament) the following week, he tied for 25th place. McIlroy tied for 41st place at the [2013 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_U.S._Open_\(golf\)), missed the cut at the [2013 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Open_Championship) and tied for 8th place as defending champion at the [2013 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_PGA_Championship).[^cite_note-109]

As a result of his downturn in form, he dropped from first to sixth in the OWGR.[^cite_note-110] McIlroy recorded his first win of the year in December, at the 2013 [Emirates Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Australian_Open). He birdied the final hole to beat Australian [Adam Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Scott_\(golfer\)) by one stroke.[^cite_note-111]

### 2014: First Open Championship, second PGA Championship title

In January, McIlroy finished runner-up again in the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Golf_Championship). He was assessed a two-shot penalty for taking an improper drop from a spectator pathway in the third round and ultimately finished one stroke behind the winner [Pablo Larrazábal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Larraz%C3%A1bal). He said afterwards: "There are many stupid rules in golf and this is one of them."[^cite_note-112]

In March, McIlroy lost in a playoff at the [Honda Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_Classic) on the PGA Tour. He held a two-shot lead entering the final round, but shot 74 (+4) and lost on the first extra hole, when [Russell Henley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Henley) made birdie.[^cite_note-113] In May, McIlroy shot a final-round 66 to overcome a seven-shot deficit and win the [2014 BMW PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_BMW_PGA_Championship) by one stroke over [Thomas Bjørn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Bj%C3%B8rn). The win was McIlroy's first on either of the two major tours in 18 months.[^cite_note-114]

At the [2014 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Open_Championship) held at [Royal Liverpool Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Liverpool_Golf_Club) in July,[^cite_note-115] McIlroy led [wire-to-wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wire-to-wire) and won by two strokes over [Rickie Fowler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rickie_Fowler) and [Sergio García](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Garc%C3%ADa) to claim the first Open Championship and third major championship of his career.[^cite_note-116] The victory put McIlroy alongside [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) as the only golfers to win both the [Silver Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Open_Championship#Silver_Medal_winners) and the [Claret Jug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claret_Jug) at the Open Championship.[^cite_note-Major_record-117]

In his next start, McIlroy won the [2014 WGC-Bridgestone Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_WGC-Bridgestone_Invitational), shooting a final-round 66 to beat [Sergio García](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Garc%C3%ADa) by two strokes. The following week, McIlroy edged out runner-up [Phil Mickelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Mickelson) by one shot to collect his fourth major championship victory with a win at the [2014 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_PGA_Championship) at [Valhalla Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valhalla_Golf_Club) in [Louisville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisville,_Kentucky).[^cite_note-118] In doing so, he joined [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) and [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) as the only three players to win four majors by age 25.[^cite_note-Major_record-117] After the victory, 18-time major champion Nicklaus said: "Rory is an unbelievable talent. I think Rory has an opportunity to win 15 or 20 majors or whatever he wants to do if he wants to keep playing. I love his swing."[^cite_note-119]

At the [2014 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Ryder_Cup) in September, McIlroy played in all five sessions. He finished with a record of 2–1–2, including a win in his Sunday singles match against [Rickie Fowler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rickie_Fowler), as Europe won by a score of 16.5–11.5.[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121]

### 2015: Four-win season



McIlroy during practice rounds at the 2015 [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship) at [East Lake Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Lake_Golf_Club)

In January, McIlroy finished runner-up for the fourth time of his career at the [Abu Dhabi Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_Golf_Championship), one stroke behind [Gary Stal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Stal). In his second tournament of the year, he won the European Tour's [Omega Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_Dubai_Desert_Classic) for a second time. At 22-under-par, he matched the tournament's scoring record set by [Stephen Gallacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Gallacher) and [Thomas Bjørn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Bj%C3%B8rn).[^cite_note-122] He missed the cut in his first start of the PGA Tour season at the [Honda Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_Classic). It was his first missed cut on the PGA Tour since the 2013 Open Championship, a streak of 22 consecutive events.[^cite_note-123]

At the [2015 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Masters_Tournament) in April, McIlroy finished in solo-fourth place, a new-best career Masters finish.[^cite_note-124] In May, he won the [2015 WGC-Cadillac Match Play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_WGC-Cadillac_Match_Play) event held at [TPC Harding Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TPC_Harding_Park) in San Francisco, defeating [Gary Woodland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Woodland) in the final.[^cite_note-125] This was his second [World Golf Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Golf_Championship) and his tenth PGA tour title.[^cite_note-126] Later in May, while playing in the [Wells Fargo Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo_Championship), McIlroy shot a course-record 61 at [Quail Hollow Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quail_Hollow_Club) in [Charlotte, North Carolina](57447.md).[^cite_note-127] He went on to win the championship for a second time, by seven strokes with a tournament record score of 21-under 267.[^cite_note-128]

A week prior to the [2015 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Open_Championship) in July, McIlroy ruptured his left [anterior talofibular ligament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anterior_talofibular_ligament) while playing soccer. This led to him withdrawing from the Open Championship and the subsequent [2015 WGC-Bridgestone Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_WGC-Bridgestone_Invitational), both tournaments where he was the defending champion.[^cite_note-129] He made his return at the [2015 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_PGA_Championship), where he made the cut and finished in 17th place.[^cite_note-pgatour_results-64] Entering the [DP World Tour Championship, Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship,_Dubai), McIlroy was leading the [Race to Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_to_Dubai) standings.[^cite_note-130] He posted a score of 21-under to win the tournament by one stroke from [Andy Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Sullivan_\(golfer\)). This was his second victory in the lucrative year-end tournament and his third Race to Dubai title in four years.[^cite_note-131]

### 2016: First FedEx Cup title

Chasing his first win of the year, McIlroy took a three-shot lead into the final round of the [2016 WGC-Cadillac Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_WGC-Cadillac_Championship) at [Trump National Doral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_National_Doral) in March, but faltered with a two-over-par 74 to finish two shots behind winner [Adam Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Scott_\(golfer\)).[^cite_note-132] At the [2016 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Masters_Tournament), McIlroy was in the final group in the third round alongside [Jordan Spieth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Spieth) but shot a five-over 77 and ultimately finished tied-10th. Afterwards, he said he was affected at the Masters by the pressure of trying to complete the [Grand Slam](224580.md).[^cite_note-133]

In May, McIlroy claimed his first victory of the year at his home [Irish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Open_\(golf\)), a tournament hosted by the Rory Foundation. He finished three strokes clear of [Russell Knox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Knox) and [Bradley Dredge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Dredge) and subsequently donated the €666,000 winner's cheque to charity.[^cite_note-134] McIlroy had qualified to represent Ireland in [the golf tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics) at the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics) in August, but withdrew due to the threat of the [Zika virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zika_virus) in Brazil and stated he would not watch the tournament.[^cite_note-135] Following the tournament, McIlroy admitted he had been surprised at the event's success and said he had been wrong to dismiss the tournament.[^cite_note-136] He later outlined his intention to compete at the [2020 Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-137]

In September, McIlroy won the [Deutsche Bank Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank_Championship) in [Norton, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norton,_Massachusetts). He produced a final-round 65 to finish two shots clear of [Paul Casey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Casey).[^cite_note-138] Two weeks later, at the 2016 [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship), McIlroy overcame a two-shot deficit after the third round to force a playoff with [Ryan Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Moore_\(golfer\)) and [Kevin Chappell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Chappell), winning the competition with a birdie at the fourth playoff hole. The result earned McIlroy his first [FedEx Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Cup) title, along with the \$10 million bonus prize.[^cite_note-139] At the [2016 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Ryder_Cup) in October, McIlroy played five matches and won three points, all when paired with [Thomas Pieters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Pieters). In the Sunday singles, he lost to [Patrick Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Reed), 1 up, due to a birdie by Reed at the last hole, as the United States defeated Europe 17–11.[^cite_note-140]

### 2017: Winless season, injury problems

At the end of 2016, Nike announced their withdrawal from the golf equipment market, releasing players from their contracts early. After spending several months trying different clubs and balls, McIlroy signed a \$100 million endorsement deal with [TaylorMade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TaylorMade).[^cite_note-MurrayGuardian-141]

McIlroy did not win during 2017, the first time he had a winless year since 2008. He was hampered throughout the season by a rib injury first sustained at the [BMW SA Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_SA_Open) in January,[^cite_note-142] where he lost out on the title in a playoff to [Graeme Storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graeme_Storm), McIlroy played a limited schedule as a result of injury, although he did record top-10 finishes at both the [2017 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Masters_Tournament) and [2017 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Open_Championship). In his final start of the season, McIlroy finished tied-63rd at the [Alfred Dunhill Links Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Dunhill_Links_Championship) in October. He said after the event that he was going to take a three-month break to recuperate from his injuries.[^cite_note-143] Having started the year at 2nd in the OWGR, McIlroy dropped to 11th by the end of the year.[^cite_note-144]

Late in the 2017 season, McIlroy parted ways with JP Fitzgerald, who had been his [caddie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caddie) since 2008,[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] and hired [Harry Diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Diamond_\(caddie\)), a former Irish amateur international golfer and childhood friend of McIlroy.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] McIlroy said he had been "inundated" with caddie requests, including from [Jim "Bones" Mackay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_%22Bones%22_Mackay) and Tiger Woods's former caddie [Steve Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Williams_\(caddie\)), but decided to opt for Diamond on a permanent basis after a successful trial period.[^cite_note-149]

### 2018–2019: First Players Championship title, second FedEx Cup

McIlroy returned after over 100 days away from professional competition at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Championship) in January 2018. Prior to the tournament, he said he had been diagnosed with a slight heart irregularity but downplayed its impact on his golf.[^cite_note-150] The following week, McIlroy finished runner-up to [Li Haotong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Haotong) in the [Omega Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_Dubai_Desert_Classic). He lost by one stroke, having led by two with five holes to play. It was the 22nd runner-up finish of his career. In March 2018, McIlroy won the [Arnold Palmer Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_Palmer_Invitational) with a final-round 64, his first win since the 2016 [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship).[^cite_note-151]

At the [2018 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Masters_Tournament), which was his tenth Masters start, McIlroy was in the final pairing on Sunday alongside [Patrick Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Reed), three strokes off the lead. McIlroy shot a two-over 74 to finish in a tie for fifth, six strokes behind the winner, Reed.[^cite_note-152] McIlroy was also in contention at the [2018 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Open_Championship), where he finished runner-up, two strokes behind [Francesco Molinari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Molinari).[^cite_note-153] In September 2018, McIlroy competed in the [2018 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Ryder_Cup). He went 2–3–0, including a loss in the Sunday singles to [Justin Thomas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Thomas), as Team Europe beat Team USA 17.5–10.5 to reclaim the Ryder Cup.[^cite_note-154]

McIlroy started 2019 on the PGA Tour in good form with five straight top-6 finishes, including a runner-up finish in February at the [2019 WGC-Mexico Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WGC-Mexico_Championship), five strokes behind [Dustin Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dustin_Johnson). McIlroy subsequently won the [2019 Players Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Players_Championship) in March with a score of −16. With the win, he joined [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) and [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) as the only players to win four majors and 15 PGA Tour titles before the age of 30.[^cite_note-155]

In June 2019, McIlroy won the [RBC Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RBC_Canadian_Open). He shot a final-round 61 to win the tournament by seven strokes and became the sixth golfer to win the career [Triple Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Crown_\(golf\)) (the PGA Tour's three oldest events being the Open Championship, the U.S. Open and the Canadian Open).[^cite_note-156] Two months later, McIlroy clinched his second [FedEx Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Cup) title by winning the [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship) at [East Lake Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Lake_Golf_Club). The winning payout was \$15 million, the largest in golf history.[^cite_note-157] He became the second player to win multiple FedEx Cups, after Tiger Woods (2007, 2009).

In November 2019, McIlroy won the [2019 WGC-HSBC Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WGC-HSBC_Champions) tournament in a playoff against [Xander Schauffele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xander_Schauffele) in [Shanghai](27643.md), China. The following month, McIlroy stated that he turned down an invitation to the European Tour's [Saudi International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_International_\(golf\)) tournament in [Riyadh](51554.md), stating that it is "not something that would excite me" and "there's a morality to it as well", in reference to Saudi Arabia's human rights record.[^cite_note-158]

### 2020–2021: Return to world number one

McIlroy began 2020 with four consecutive top-5 finishes on the PGA Tour, which, along with his four wins in the previous calendar year, brought him back to world number one in the OWGR for the first time since 2015.[^cite_note-159] Amidst the suspension of professional tournaments due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), McIlroy, alongside [Dustin Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dustin_Johnson), secured a win in a charity skins game in May 2020 played under the nearest-the-pin shot rule, as golf returned to television after nine weeks. The event raised around £4 million for relief efforts.[^cite_note-160] In July, McIlroy lost his spot at world number one to [Jon Rahm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Rahm).[^cite_note-161]

At the delayed [2020 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._Open_\(golf\)) held in September, McIlroy finished in a tie for 8th place. He also recorded a top-5 finish at the [2020 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Masters_Tournament), which was delayed until November. In January 2021, McIlroy held the 54-hole lead at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Championship). A final round 72 saw him finish in third place, five shots behind eventual winner [Tyrrell Hatton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrrell_Hatton).[^cite_note-162] In May 2021, McIlroy won the [Wells Fargo Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo_Championship) at [Quail Hollow Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quail_Hollow_Club) by one stroke, which was his third win at Quail Hollow and his 19th PGA Tour victory.[^cite_note-163]

McIlroy represented [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland_at_the_2020_Summer_Olympics) at the [Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_at_the_2020_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_individual) in August 2021. He tied for third after 72 holes and lost in a seven-man playoff for the bronze medal.[^cite_note-164] In September, he played on the European team in the [2021 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Ryder_Cup) at [Whistling Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whistling_Straits) in [Kohler, Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohler,_Wisconsin). The US team won 19–9 and McIlroy went 1–3–0, including a win in his Sunday singles match against [Xander Schauffele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xander_Schauffele).

In October 2021, McIlroy won the [CJ Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJ_Cup). He shot 62-66 on the weekend to win by one stroke over [Collin Morikawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collin_Morikawa). This was his 20th PGA Tour victory, earning him life membership.[^cite_note-165] In November, McIlroy held the 54-hole lead at the [DP World Tour Championship, Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship,_Dubai), as he sought to become the first player to win the event for a third time. He faltered with a final-round 74 and ultimately finished five shots behind the winner Morikawa.[^cite_note-166]

### 2022: Top 10 at all four majors, third FedEx Cup title

In January, McIlroy was in contention to win the [Slync.io Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slync.io_Dubai_Desert_Classic) for the third time in his career but hit his second shot into the water on the final hole and missed out on a playoff for the title by one stroke.[^cite_note-167] At the [2022 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Masters_Tournament) In April, McIlroy shot a bogey-free 64 in the final round to finish as runner-up, a new-best finish at the Masters of his career.[^cite_note-168] In May, McIlroy held the first-round lead at the [2022 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_PGA_Championship) after opening with a 65. He ultimately finished 8th.[^cite_note-169]

In June, McIlroy defended his title at the [RBC Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RBC_Canadian_Open), shooting a final-round 62 to win by two shots ahead of [Tony Finau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Finau).[^cite_note-170] The following week, he finished tied-5th at the [2022 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_U.S._Open_\(golf\)). In July, McIlroy was tied for the lead after 54 holes at the [2022 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Open_Championship). He shot a final-round 70 to finish third, two strokes behind [Cameron Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Smith_\(golfer\)). He thus finished [top-10 in all four majors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men's_major_golf_championships#Top_ten_finishes_in_all_four_modern_majors_in_one_season) for the first time in his career.[^cite_note-171]

In August, McIlroy won his third [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship), by one stroke from [Scottie Scheffler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottie_Scheffler) and [Im Sung-jae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Im_Sung-jae), to claim his third [FedEx Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Cup) victory. This victory surpassed [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods)'s two successes and earned McIlroy an additional \$18 million bonus.[^cite_note-172] In September, he finished tied-second at the [BMW PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_PGA_Championship), losing to [Shane Lowry](22834302.md) by one shot.[^cite_note-173]

In October, McIlroy successfully defended the [CJ Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJ_Cup) at [Congaree Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congaree_Golf_Club) in South Carolina. This victory also saw him return to number one in the [Official World Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_World_Golf_Ranking), overtaking [Scottie Scheffler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottie_Scheffler).[^cite_note-174] In November, McIlroy entered the [DP World Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship) on the European Tour sitting in first place on the [DP World Tour Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Rankings). A fourth-place finish was enough for McIlroy to maintain his position at the top of the rankings. It was also his fourth [Harry Vardon Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Vardon_Trophy) win.[^cite_note-175]

### 2023: Continued success, fifth Race to Dubai title

McIlroy started off the year by winning the [Hero Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero_Dubai_Desert_Classic) In January. He birdied the final two holes to beat [Patrick Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Reed) by one shot.[^cite_note-176] McIlroy missed the cut at the [2023 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Masters_Tournament) in April,[^cite_note-177] and finished tied-7th at the [2023 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_PGA_Championship) in May.&#x20;

At the [2023 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_U.S._Open_\(golf\)) in June, McIlroy finished solo-second, one shot behind [Wyndham Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyndham_Clark).[^cite_note-178] In the final round of the U.S. Open, McIlroy received an incorrect drop after officials determined his ball was "plugged in the ground" and then misidentified the nearest point of relief. The USGA later acknowledged their error, but since McIlroy’s ball was dropped within 18 inches of the correct spot and he earned no advantage from its placement, no penalty was given.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] In July, McIlroy won the [Genesis Scottish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_Scottish_Open), one stroke ahead of [Robert MacIntyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_MacIntyre).[^cite_note-181] The following week, at the [2023 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Open_Championship), he finished tied-sixth.[^cite_note-182]

In September, McIlroy played on the European team in the [2023 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ryder_Cup) at [Marco Simone Golf and Country Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Simone_Golf_and_Country_Club) in [Rome](25458.md), Italy. The European team won 16.5–11.5 and McIlroy was the top points scorer with a 4–1–0 record, including a win in his Sunday singles match against [Sam Burns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Burns). In November, McIlroy won his fifth [Race to Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_to_Dubai) title; he finished tied-22nd at the [DP World Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship), but had already secured the Race to Dubai title before the tournament started. This moved McIlroy to third for most season-long titles won on the European Tour, behind [Seve Ballesteros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seve_Ballesteros)'s six and [Colin Montgomerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Montgomerie)'s eight.[^cite_note-183]

### 2024: Four-win season, U.S. Open loss

McIlroy opened his season at the inaugural [Dubai Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Invitational) in January. He led by one shot with one hole remaining, before hitting his tee shot on the final hole into the water. He bogeyed the hole while [Tommy Fleetwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Fleetwood) made birdie to win by one.[^cite_note-184] He defended the [Hero Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero_Dubai_Desert_Classic) the following week, his fourth win in the tournament overall. Trailing by 11 shots entering Saturday, he shot a nine-under 63 to propel him into contention before a final round two-under 70 gave him a one shot win over [Adrian Meronk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Meronk).[^cite_note-185]

After a tepid start to [the season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_PGA_Tour) on the PGA Tour, including a T22 finish at the [2024 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Masters_Tournament), McIlroy won his first title of the season in April at the [Zurich Classic of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zurich_Classic_of_New_Orleans), a team event where he competed alongside Ryder Cup teammate [Shane Lowry](22834302.md). They won in a playoff over [Chad Ramey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad_Ramey) and [Martin Trainer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Trainer).[^cite_note-186] This was McIlroy's 25th win on the PGA Tour.[^cite_note-187] In his next start, McIlroy won the [Wells Fargo Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo_Championship) for the fourth time in his career. He closed with a 6-under 65, playing the final 11 holes in 6-under and won by five strokes over [Xander Schauffele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xander_Schauffele).[^cite_note-188]

During the [2024 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_U.S._Open_\(golf\)), McIlroy entered the final round in second-place, three strokes behind the lead. He erased the deficit and had a two-stroke lead over [Bryson DeChambeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryson_DeChambeau) with four holes to play, but bogeyed three of the final four holes, including missed putts from inside four feet on both the 16th and 18th holes, to finish one stroke behind DeChambeau.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] At the [2024 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Open_Championship), McIlroy shot 78-75 and missed the cut, extending his major championship drought to ten years.[^cite_note-191]

In September, after the [Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_Championship), McIlroy turned his attention to the [European Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Tour), recording back-to-back runner-up finishes at the [Amgen Irish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amgen_Irish_Open)[^cite_note-192] and the [BMW PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_PGA_Championship), in which he lost in a playoff to [Billy Horschel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Horschel).[^cite_note-193] In November, at the season's penultimate event, he finished tied-third at the [Abu Dhabi HSBC Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_HSBC_Championship).[^cite_note-194] The following week, he won the [DP World Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship), also claiming the [Race to Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_to_Dubai) title for the sixth time in his career.[^cite_note-195]

### 2025: Completion of career grand slam, sixth Ryder Cup win



McIlroy at the [Travelers Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travelers_Championship) in June 2025

In his first PGA Tour event of 2025, McIlroy won the [AT\&T Pebble Beach Pro-Am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT\&T_Pebble_Beach_Pro-Am), finishing with a score of −21, beating [Shane Lowry](22834302.md) by two strokes.[^cite_note-196] At the [2025 Players Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Players_Championship) in March, McIlroy shot a final-round 68 to tie for first with [J. J. Spaun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._J._Spaun) and entered a three-hole aggregate playoff. McIlroy won the playoff with an aggregate score of +1 to claim his second Players Championship title.[^cite_note-197] Two weeks later, he finished tied-fifth at the [Texas Children's Houston Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Children's_Houston_Open) and earned \$337,844, which took his career earnings on the PGA Tour to \$100,046,906. This made McIlroy the second man to reach \$100 million in official PGA Tour earnings, joining Tiger Woods who crossed the mark in 2012.[^cite_note-198]

In April, at the [2025 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Masters_Tournament), McIlroy held a two-stroke lead after 54 holes, which was his first 54-hole outright lead at a major since the [2014 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_PGA_Championship).[^cite_note-199] He shot a 1-over 73 in the final round while [Justin Rose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Rose) birdied six of his final eight holes to force a playoff.[^cite_note-200] McIlroy birdied the first playoff hole to win his first Masters and complete the career [grand slam](224580.md). He became the sixth player, after [Gene Sarazen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Sarazen), [Ben Hogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Hogan), [Gary Player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Player), [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) and [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods), to win each of the four major championships in the modern era.[^cite_note-201]

In his next major championship start, McIlroy tied for 47th at the [2025 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_PGA_Championship), held at [Quail Hollow Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quail_Hollow_Club), a course where he had previously recorded four wins. He then missed his first cut of the year at the [RBC Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RBC_Canadian_Open), shooting rounds of 71-78.[^cite_note-202] After the [2025 U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_U.S._Open_\(golf\)), where he finished tied-19th,[^cite_note-203] McIlroy stated he had lost motivation since winning the Masters: "I climbed my Everest in April, and I think after you do something like that, you've got to make your way back down, and you've got to look for another mountain to climb."[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

In July, McIlroy finished runner-up at the [Genesis Scottish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_Scottish_Open), two shots behind [Chris Gotterup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Gotterup).[^cite_note-206] The following week, McIlroy was the home favourite in the [2025 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Open_Championship) held at [Royal Portrush Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Portrush_Golf_Club). He finished tied-seventh.[^cite_note-207] At the [Amgen Irish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amgen_Irish_Open) in September, McIlroy holed a 27-foot (8.2 m) putt for eagle on the final hole to match the 17-under 271 posted by [Joakim Lagergren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joakim_Lagergren) and enter a playoff. McIlroy made birdie on the third extra hole to win his national open for the second time in his career.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209]

McIlroy represented Europe at the [2025 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Ryder_Cup), held at [Bethpage Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethpage_Black) in September. He had a 3–1–1 record, including a loss in the Sunday singles against world number one [Scottie Scheffler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottie_Scheffler), as Europe defeated the United States by a score of 15–13, which was the first away victory in a Ryder Cup since [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Ryder_Cup). This marked the sixth time he won the event with team Europe.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] In November, McIlroy made eagle on the final hole of the [DP World Tour Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DP_World_Tour_Championship) to force a playoff against [Matt Fitzpatrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Fitzpatrick). McIlroy lost the title to Fitzpatrick on the first playoff hole, but the runner-up finish secured his seventh [Race to Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_to_Dubai) title, overtaking [Seve Ballesteros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seve_Ballesteros)'s tally of six and moving within one of the record held by [Colin Montgomerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Montgomerie).[^cite_note-212]

### 2026: Second Masters title

McIlroy finished runner-up at the [Genesis Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_Invitational) in February, one stroke behind [Jacob Bridgeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Bridgeman).[^cite_note-213] As defending champion at the [2026 Players Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Players_Championship) in March, McIlroy placed tied-46th.[^cite_note-214] He struggled prior to the Players with a back injury which had forced him to withdraw from the [Arnold Palmer Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_Palmer_Invitational) a week earlier.[^cite_note-215]

In his title defence at the [2026 Masters Tournament](82215987.md), McIlroy opened with rounds of 67-65 to total 12-under and establish a six-stroke lead. This broke the record for largest 36-hole lead in Masters history, surpassing the five-shot margin first set by [Harry Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Cooper_\(golfer\)) in [1936](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936_Masters_Tournament).[^cite_note-216] McIlroy's lead vanished after a 73 in the third round and he was reduced to co-leader alongside [Cameron Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Young).[^cite_note-217] McIlroy closed with a 1-under 71 to claim his second Masters title, finishing one stroke ahead of [Scottie Scheffler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottie_Scheffler). With the victory, he joined [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus) ([1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Masters_Tournament)), [Nick Faldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Faldo) ([1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Masters_Tournament)) and [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) ([2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Masters_Tournament)) as the only players to successfully defend their titles at the Masters.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] McIlroy also drew level with Faldo, [Phil Mickelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Mickelson) and [Lee Trevino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Trevino) with six major championships.[^cite_note-220] The [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS) broadcast of McIlroy's victory averaged 14 million viewers, which was the highest viewership since the [2015 Masters Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Masters_Tournament).[^cite_note-221]

### Awards

At the 2005 *[Belfast Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belfast_Telegraph)* Sports Awards, McIlroy received the Young Player of the Year award.[^cite_note-222] He also won the 2008 George Best Breakthrough Prize and the 2009 *Belfast Telegraph* Sports Star of the Year award.[^cite_note-223]

In recognition of his win at the 2011 U.S. Open, McIlroy was named the 2011 [RTÉ Sports Person of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT%C3%89_Sports_Person_of_the_Year)[^cite_note-224] and [BBC Northern Ireland Sports Personality of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Northern_Ireland_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-225] He also received the [Laureus World Sports Award for Breakthrough of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award_for_Breakthrough_of_the_Year),[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227] and was appointed [Member of the Order of the British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_Order_of_the_British_Empire) (MBE) in the [2012 New Year Honours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_New_Year_Honours), for services to sport.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-mbe-229]

In 2012, McIlroy won the [PGA Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Player_of_the_Year) and [PGA Tour Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour_Player_of_the_Year) awards, as well as the [Vardon Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vardon_Trophy) and [Byron Nelson Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vardon_Trophy).[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] He was also voted the [European Tour Golfer of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Tour_Golfer_of_the_Year) and [European Tour Players' Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Tour_Players'_Player_of_the_Year) in 2012. He repeated this award sweep in 2014.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] Also in 2014, he was named RTÉ Sports Person of the Year for a second time,[^cite_note-234] and BBC Northern Ireland Sports Personality of the Year for a third time.[^cite_note-235]

McIlroy has won the [Mark H. McCormack Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_H._McCormack_Award) for leading the [Official World Golf Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_World_Golf_Ranking) for the most weeks in a calendar year three times: 2012, 2014 and 2015.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] In 2019, he won the PGA Tour Player of the Year award and the Vardon Trophy for the third time.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] In 2022, McIlroy won the Vardon Trophy for a fourth time. His scoring average of 68.67 was the lowest since Tiger Woods's 68.05 in 2009.[^cite_note-241] In 2024, McIlroy won the European Tour Player of the Year award, now renamed the [Seve Ballesteros Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seve_Ballesteros_Award), for the fourth time in his career.[^cite_note-242]

McIlroy won [BBC Sports Personality of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year_Award) in [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BBC_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year_Award), topping the public vote;[^cite_note-243] he had previously been shortlisted in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_BBC_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year_Award) and [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BBC_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year_Award), finishing as runner-up in 2014.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] McIlroy was also named the 2025 [RTÉ Sports Person of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT%C3%89_Sports_Person_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-246] This made him the first person to win both these awards in the same year since [Barry McGuigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_McGuigan) in 1985.[^cite_note-247]

## Technique and coaches



McIlroy in the process of hitting a tee shot with his driver

Despite his modest stature at 5 feet 9 inches (1.75 m), McIlroy has consistently been among the longest [drivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drive_\(golf\)) in professional golf throughout his career.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] He had a "grip-it-and-rip-it" style with a long, fluid swing when he first came out on tour.[^cite_note-mens-250][^cite_note-251] McIlroy developed back problems by the age of 19, requiring the use of heavy strapping and [anti-inflammatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-inflammatory) medication while playing.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] He was warned by a doctor that he likely would develop a [stress fracture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stress_fracture) in his back if he did not change his approach.[^cite_note-254] McIlroy began to work with exercise physiologist Stephen McGregor in 2010,[^cite_note-\:2-255] who noted imbalances in McIlroy's body: "He couldn't stand on one leg. He didn't have support and stability in his shoulders. ... He was very arm-speed-dominated in his golf swing, as a lot of young players are, trying to generate as much speed as they can to launch the golf ball."[^cite_note-mens-250]

McGregor, along with McIlroy's long-time coach [Michael Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Bannon),[^cite_note-256] set about rebuilding McIlroy's swing. He started a strict workout regimen, focused on developing strength in his legs and core to generate speed from the ground up and reduce strain on the back. As a result, his clubhead speed became faster than before.[^cite_note-mens-250] McIlroy said in 2017 that he had a [degenerative disc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Degenerative_disc) in his [lumbar vertebrae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lumbar_vertebrae), adding: "I've been swinging a golf club since I was two years old. That's millions of swings over time, and that takes its toll." He credited McGregor as having the biggest impact of anyone on his golf career, stating that McGregor's guidance had helped him to take better care of his body and extend his career.[^cite_note-\:2-255] As of 2025, McIlroy is capable of producing over 190 miles per hour (310 km/h) ball speed.[^cite_note-257]

McIlroy struggled with his [putting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putt_\(golf\)) at the start of his career and began to work with specialist coach Paul Hurrion in 2008, who stated that McIlroy was overly reliant on [hand-eye coordination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand-eye_coordination) when putting.[^cite_note-putting-258][^cite_note-davestockton-259] McIlroy also was introduced to Bob Rotella's book *Putting Out of Your Mind* in late 2009.[^cite_note-putting-258] McIlroy later obtained putting instruction from former PGA Tour player [Dave Stockton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Stockton) and credited him after winning the [2012 PGA Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_PGA_Championship) for the improvements in his putting.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] McIlroy underwent laser eye surgery in 2015, having previously used [contact lenses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_lenses) while on the course. He said: "I've always felt I struggled reading greens. I've always struggled with my eyes, especially in the summer with hayfever, when you're rubbing your eyes and sometimes things would get under the contact lens," and that after the surgery he had "better than [20/20 vision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20/20_vision)".[^cite_note-262] Since 2018, former PGA Tour player [Brad Faxon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Faxon) has been McIlroy's putting coach. Faxon was known as one of the best putters on tour during his career.[^cite_note-263]

## Public image

McIlroy has been regarded as one of the most marketable athletes in the world since early on in his career.[^cite_note-seattle-pi-87][^cite_note-264] He was an ambassador for hotel chain [Jumeirah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumeirah_\(hotel_chain\)) from 2007 to 2012,[^cite_note-265] and signed a multimillion-dollar sponsorship agreement with [Santander Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santander_Bank) in 2011.[^cite_note-266] *[SportsPro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SportsPro)* rated him at third, behind [Neymar](15326144.md) and [Lionel Messi](2150841.md), in their 2013 list of the world's most marketable athletes.[^cite_note-267] In 2013, McIlroy signed a 10-year endorsement deal with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) worth up to \$250 million, whereby he agreed to be a brand ambassador for their golf clubs, balls and apparel.[^cite_note-268] According to Simon Chadwick, professor of sport business strategy and marketing at [Coventry University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coventry_University), Nike viewed McIlroy as their successor to [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods), who was struggling on and off the course at the time.[^cite_note-269] Also in that year, McIlroy signed endorsement deals with [Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bose_Corporation) and [Omega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_SA).[^cite_note-270] In 2015, McIlroy became the namesake for the [EA Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports) video game *[Rory McIlroy PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rory_McIlroy_PGA_Tour)*, replacing Tiger Woods, who had been the previous namesake for [the series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour_\(video_game_series\)) from 1998 to 2013.[^cite_note-271] In 2017, McIlroy signed a 10-year, \$200-million contract extension with Nike for apparel only, after Nike exited the golf equipment business, allowing him to additionally sign a 10-year, \$100-million equipment deal with [TaylorMade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TaylorMade-Adidas) to use their clubs, ball and bag.[^cite_note-MurrayGuardian-141]

After the emergence of [LIV Golf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIV_Golf) in 2022, McIlroy became a vocal opponent of the Saudi-funded league and some of the prominent individuals associated with it, such as [Greg Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Norman) and [Phil Mickelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Mickelson).[^cite_note-272] *[Sports Business Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Business_Journal)* stated that McIlroy "took on the unofficial role as face of the PGA Tour in its battle with LIV Golf".[^cite_note-sbj-273] McIlroy criticised the league for having "ripped apart" the game of professional golf.[^cite_note-274] Before the start of the [Hero Dubai Desert Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero_Dubai_Desert_Classic) in January 2023, McIlroy was approached by LIV golfer [Patrick Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Reed) while on the practice range. He refused to talk to Reed, stating afterwards when asked in a press conference that Reed had sent a lawyer to serve him a [subpoena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subpoena) on the Christmas Eve prior as part of an [anti-trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-trust) lawsuit.[^cite_note-275] In June 2023, after the PGA Tour announced a framework agreement for a potential merger with LIV Golf, McIlroy said that he felt like a "sacrificial lamb" due to his support of the PGA Tour.[^cite_note-276] He maintained that "If LIV Golf was the last place to play golf on Earth, I would retire."[^cite_note-277] McIlroy softened his stance against LIV Golf in 2024, stating that he had been "a little judgemental" towards golfers who joined LIV, adding "I wouldn't say I've lost the fight against LIV, but I've just accepted the fact that this is part of our sport now."[^cite_note-278] Following the announcement in 2026 that Saudi Arabia's [Public Investment Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Investment_Fund) would withdraw its multibillion-dollar funding of LIV Golf, McIlroy said: "it doesn't mean that LIV is going to go away. ... But when one of the wealthiest sovereign wealth funds in the world thinks that you're too expensive for them, that sort of says something."[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]

## Business endeavours

When turning professional in 2007, McIlroy signed with [International Sports Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Sports_Management) (ISM), an agency founded by former professional golfer [Chubby Chandler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chubby_Chandler).
