McIlroy left ISM in 2011,[^cite_note-281] and stated he felt like he was being "led down the wrong path" by Chandler, who advised McIlroy to give up his PGA Tour card in 2010 and prioritise the European Tour.[^cite_note-282] McIlroy then signed with Horizon Sports Management, following the lead of his friend [Graeme McDowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graeme_McDowell) who had left ISM to join Horizon.[^cite_note-283] McIlroy split from Horizon in 2013.[^cite_note-284] He had agreed a contract extension with the agency in March 2013, which was set to run until 2017.[^cite_note-285] In October 2013, he described the contract as "unconscionable" and said he had been coaxed into signing it. He filed suit against Horizon, claiming they had prioritised their own financial interests to his detriment.[^cite_note-286] Horizon countersued, alleging breach of contract and unpaid fees. A legal battle followed, which ended with an out-of-court settlement in 2015.[^cite_note-287] *[The Irish Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irish_Times)* reported that McIlroy paid the firm in excess of \$25 million plus costs to settle the dispute.[^cite_note-288]

After splitting from Horizon in 2013, McIlroy created a new management company, Rory McIlroy Management Services Ltd.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] Initially headed by former [Irish Life and Permanent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Life_and_Permanent) executive Donal Casey as managing director alongside McIlroy's father Gerry as a director, it manages the royalty payments from McIlroy's various endorsements.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] The company is based in [Dublin](8504.md).[^cite_note-294] In 2017, McIlroy ranked sixth in [*Forbes*'s list of the world's highest-paid athletes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_athletes), having earned \$50 million, of which \$34 million came from endorsements.[^cite_note-295]

In 2019, McIlroy founded Symphony Ventures, a [venture capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital) firm, alongside his long-time agent Sean O'Flaherty.[^cite_note-sbj-273] Headquartered in Dublin, it focuses on the healthcare, sports and technology sectors.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] McIlroy began wearing a [Whoop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whoop_\(company\)) band in 2019 and was among the investors in a \$100-million Series E financing round for the company in 2020.[^cite_note-298] He joined the investment group of the [Alpine racing team](65240620.md) of [Formula One](10854.md) in 2023,[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] and the ticket marketplace [TickPick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TickPick) in 2024.[^cite_note-sbj-273][^cite_note-301] McIlroy founded TMRW Sports in 2022, alongside [Tiger Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_Woods) and former [Golf Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_Channel) executive [Mike McCarley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_McCarley).[^cite_note-302] The company's first project was [TGL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TGL_\(golf_league\)), an indoor golf league which secured a broadcasting deal with [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN). A funding round in 2024 valued TMRW Sports at \$500 million.[^cite_note-303]

In May 2025, McIlroy partnered with private equity firm [TPG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TPG_Inc.) to form TPG Sports, an investment fund targeting the sports sector.[^cite_note-304] In December 2025, McIlroy announced a new joint venture with Golf Channel parent company [Versant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versant) known as Firethorn Productions (named after the 15th hole at Augusta National), and an extension of his contract with the company through 2038.[^cite_note-305]

## Personal life

McIlroy was raised [Catholic](606848.md) and has self-identified as Irish,[^cite_note-mcilroytimeline-306] Northern Irish,[^cite_note-mcilroytimeline-306] and British.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308][^cite_note-BBCidentity-309] He stated in 2010 that he carries a [British passport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_passport),[^cite_note-310] although he usually is reluctant to discuss his nationality at length.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] During [the Troubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Troubles), McIlroy's great-uncle Joe McIlroy was shot to death by the [Ulster Volunteer Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulster_Volunteer_Force) in a sectarian attack at his home in 1972, after he moved his Catholic family into the predominantly-Protestant area of [Orangefield, Belfast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orangefield,_Belfast).[^cite_note-\:12-313][^cite_note-SiebertNYT-12][^cite_note-314] Despite this family trauma, McIlroy's parents refused to become embittered.[^cite_note-\:9-7][^cite_note-\:12-313] McIlroy attended a [mixed school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_education_in_Northern_Ireland) that was majority Protestant and stated his Catholic background "was never an issue". Regarding the Troubles, McIlroy said in 2012: "I haven't talked about it with my parents. I don't want to be defined by that. It's not how my generation thinks."[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

As golf was named an Olympic sport for the first time since 1904 at the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics), McIlroy was regularly questioned whether he would represent [Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Britain_at_the_Olympics) or [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland_at_the_Olympics). He stated in 2013: "I feel Northern Irish and obviously being from Northern Ireland you have a connection to Ireland and a connection to the UK. If I could and there was a Northern Irish team I'd play for Northern Ireland. Play for one side or the other – or not play at all because I may upset too many people ... Those are my three options I'm considering very carefully."[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] On the eve of the 2014 [Irish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Open_\(golf\)), he declared that he would represent Ireland at the Olympics.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]

In 2009, McIlroy bought a house in [Moneyreagh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moneyreagh), a small village south of Belfast.[^cite_note-321] The house sat on a 13-acre plot of land, where he had a custom-made practice facility and a scaled-down [association football](10568.md) pitch.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323] McIlroy hired security guards for the property after he won the 2011 U.S. Open, as the increased attention led to strangers driving up his driveway. He said he began to feel like he was living in a goldfish bowl.[^cite_note-324] In September 2012, the house was put up for sale for a price of £2 million.[^cite_note-325] In December 2012, McIlroy purchased a \$10-million property in [Palm Beach Gardens, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Beach_Gardens,_Florida), located close to [Jack Nicklaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nicklaus)'s Bear's Club.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327] McIlroy has also maintained residences in [Monaco](19261.md) and [Dubai](211583.md).[^cite_note-328] In 2024, McIlroy took delivery of a [Gulfstream G650ER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulfstream_G650ER), a private jet. He previously owned a [Bombardier Challenger 605](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Challenger_605).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] In 2025, McIlroy moved into a home in the [Wentworth Estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wentworth_Estate) in Surrey.[^cite_note-331]

McIlroy is a fan of [Manchester United F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.)[^cite_note-332] Following his 2014 Open Championship win at [Royal Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Liverpool_Golf_Club), he made reference to his support for [Liverpool's rivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.%E2%80%93Manchester_United_F.C._rivalry) during his acceptance speech, prompting light-hearted boos from the crowd.[^cite_note-333] McIlroy is also a fan of the [Northern Ireland national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_national_football_team). In his spare time, he played football himself. McIlroy injured his ankle ligaments in 2015, while playing with friends, forcing him to withdraw from the [2015 Open Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Open_Championship).[^cite_note-334] He said in December of that year: "I really can't be doing silly things like playing football in the middle of the season ... I won't be making those mistakes again."[^cite_note-335] McIlroy is a fan of [Ulster Rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulster_Rugby) and has interrupted his busy golf schedule to attend matches at [Ravenhill Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ravenhill_Stadium) in Belfast.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] McIlroy said in 2023 that he is a fan of the [Buffalo Bills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo_Bills),[^cite_note-338] as his wife Erica is originally from [Irondequoit, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irondequoit,_New_York). McIlroy stated: "It certainly makes it easier to root for the Bills when [Josh Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Allen) is throwing the football."[^cite_note-339]

In 2018, McIlroy appeared on an episode of [Amazon Prime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Prime) show *[The Grand Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Grand_Tour)*, racing against [Paris Hilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Hilton) in the "Celebrity Face-Off" segment of the show.[^cite_note-340] McIlroy featured in the sports documentary series *[Full Swing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_Swing_\(2023_TV_series\))*, which premiered on Netflix in 2023.[^cite_note-341] McIlroy made a cameo appearance in the 2025 film *[Happy Gilmore 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Happy_Gilmore_2)*.[^cite_note-342] He also made a cameo appearance in *[The Devil Wears Prada 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Devil_Wears_Prada_2)* (2026).[^cite_note-343]

### Relationships

McIlroy dated Danish tennis player [Caroline Wozniacki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Wozniacki) from 2011 to 2014.[^cite_note-344] They became engaged in December 2013.[^cite_note-345] McIlroy ended the engagement in May 2014:[^cite_note-346] "The problem is mine. The wedding invitations issued at the weekend made me realise that I wasn't ready for all that marriage entails. I wish Caroline all the happiness she deserves and thank her for the great times we've had."[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348]

McIlroy started dating Erica Stoll, a former [PGA of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_of_America) employee, in 2015.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] In December that year, they became engaged while on holiday in [Paris](22989.md).[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] They married in April 2017 at [Ashford Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashford_Castle) in [Cong, County Mayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cong,_County_Mayo).[^cite_note-353] The couple had a daughter in September 2020.[^cite_note-354] In May 2024, McIlroy filed for divorce in [Palm Beach County, Florida](73647.md).[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] By June 2024, the divorce filing was withdrawn, with McIlroy stating: "We have resolved our differences and look forward to a new beginning."[^cite_note-357]

### Philanthropy

McIlroy became an ambassador for [UNICEF Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNICEF_Ireland) in 2011,[^cite_note-358] and visited [Haiti](13373.md) with UNICEF in June of that year.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] He cancelled a trip to Haiti with UNICEF in 2013 as it conflicted with his participation in the [Valero Texas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valero_Texas_Open). McIlroy's management team Horizon then donated \$166,000 of his money to UNICEF without his knowledge, to "avoid any bad publicity".[^cite_note-\:1-361][^cite_note-362] McIlroy had been advised by his then-partner Caroline Wozniacki against donating to [NGOs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NGOs) such as UNICEF and instead to create a foundation. McIlroy reversed the donation, and was upset that his management team had made the transaction without his approval.[^cite_note-\:1-361][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364]

In 2013, McIlroy created The Rory Foundation, with a stated goal of supporting children's charities.[^cite_note-down-365] Through the foundation, McIlroy pledged £1 million in 2014 to the Cancer Fund for Children in [Newcastle, County Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newcastle,_County_Down).[^cite_note-366] During 2016, the foundation dispensed a total of £1.15 million to charities in Ireland and the United Kingdom, of which £862,543 was donated directly by McIlroy.[^cite_note-367] The foundation shut down in December 2018, with a spokeswoman of the foundation stating that McIlroy "is adopting a private philanthropic policy".[^cite_note-down-365] It was announced in 2023 that McIlroy had paid €1 million towards the construction of a Cancer Fund for Children lodge in [Cong, County Mayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cong,_County_Mayo).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369]

  [^cite_note-EGABoys-25]

  [^cite_note-380]

- [St Andrews Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Andrews_Trophy) (representing Great Britain & Ireland): 2006 (winners)

- [Walker Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walker_Cup) (representing Great Britain & Ireland): [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Walker_Cup)

- [Bonallack Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonallack_Trophy) (representing Europe): 2006 (winners)
